I was up late working on it, but I don’t really know if I needed less sleep, exactly. I just remember staying busy and not wanting to stop.\n\nDoctor: During that time, did you hear voices or sounds that other people could not hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I can’t really say I heard voices or sounds that weren’t there. Nothing like that that I noticed.\n\nDoctor: Before this recent time, have you ever had another period when you felt unusually energized or driven like this?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Not exactly sure when you’d count it, but I’ve had times where I just couldn’t sit in the chair anymore. I’d be up and moving, going from the garden to the darkroom to the porch, carrying tools around, fixing things before anybody even asked me to.\n\nDoctor: When you have one of these energized times, does it usually last at least a week?\n\nPatient: Yes, it’s been more than a week. This has been going on about three months now, since the remodeling started and the whole house got turned upside down.\n\nDoctor: During that time, did you have any beliefs that other people said were clearly not true?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people have told me I’m mixed up about it, but I don’t think I am. I’m sure this neighborhood was picked for my last big civic art piece. Little things show it, like trucks slowing down out front or neighbors looking over like they know what’s going on. To me that proves they know too.\n\nDoctor: Because of this period, have you had to stay in a hospital for mental health reasons?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that… I don’t think I’ve had to stay in the hospital for it, at least not that I can clearly remember.\n\nDoctor: During this recent time, were you admitted to a hospital because of these symptoms?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I don’t remember being admitted to a hospital for it during this recent time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these unusual beliefs at times when you were not feeling especially energized or driven?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Before I got all charged up with the remodeling plans, I wasn’t seeing special meaning in the trucks or the neighbors looking over. Like I had to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least a couple of weeks when you felt depressed or lost interest in almost everything?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve had a couple weeks like that, at least not that I can clearly point to. I’ve had bad days, sure, but I don’t know about losing interest in everything for that long.\n\nDoctor: During that time, did you feel unusually important or specially chosen for a big purpose?\n\nPatient: Yes… I did feel like that. I felt like I was the one who could make that old Schiller Park house into something people would come see. Like nobody else really understood lights and pictures the way I do, and I had to be the one to do it.\n\nDoctor: During that time, did you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I was up late working on it, but I don’t really know if I needed less sleep, exactly. I just remember staying busy and not wanting to stop.\n\nDoctor: During that time, did you hear voices or sounds that other people could not hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I can’t really say I heard voices or sounds that weren’t there. Nothing like that that I noticed.\n\nDoctor: Before this recent time, have you ever had another period when you felt unusually energized or driven like this?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Not exactly sure when you’d count it, but I’ve had times where I just couldn’t sit in the chair anymore. I’d be up and moving, going from the garden to the darkroom to the porch, carrying tools around, fixing things before anybody even asked me to.\n\nDoctor: When you have one of these energized times, does it usually last at least a week?\n\nPatient: Yes, it’s been more than a week. This has been going on about three months now, since the remodeling started and the whole house got turned upside down.\n\nDoctor: During that time, did you have any beliefs that other people said were clearly not true?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people have told me I’m mixed up about it, but I don’t think I am. I’m sure this neighborhood was picked for my last big civic art piece. Little things show it, like trucks slowing down out front or neighbors looking over like they know what’s going on. To me that proves they know too.\n\nDoctor: Because of this period, have you had to stay in a hospital for mental health reasons?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that… I don’t think I’ve had to stay in the hospital for it, at least not that I can clearly remember.\n\nDoctor: During this recent time, were you admitted to a hospital because of these symptoms?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I don’t remember being admitted to a hospital for it during this recent time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these unusual beliefs at times when you were not feeling especially energized or driven?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Before I got all charged up with the remodeling plans, I wasn’t seeing special meaning in the trucks or the neighbors looking over. That only started after I felt driven to make the house into that big civic project.\n\nDoctor: At any time in your life, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve had down days like anybody, but I can’t say I remember a full two weeks where I was depressed most of the day nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: During this recent energized period, did your behavior cause serious problems in your life, like major trouble at home, work, or with money?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s caused problems at home. The house has gotten hard for my wife and me to live in because I’ve got materials and half-finished setups taking over rooms we need. And some people I care about have been staying away from me after the arguments.\n\nDoctor: During this recent period, have you felt unusually cheerful or unusually irritable compared with your normal self?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days I’ve felt kind of charged up, almost joyful, like I’m young again. But I also get irritated fast—if my wife or the contractors question what I’m doing, I get hot and start yelling.\n\nDoctor: At any point in your life, have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you lost interest or pleasure in almost everything you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I can’t really think of a time like that, where for two weeks or more I didn’t care about almost anything I usually enjoy. Nothing that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: At any time in your life, have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I’ve had bad days, sure, but I can’t remember a stretch of two weeks where I felt depressed most of the day nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: During this recent energized period, did your symptoms make you unable to carry out your usual responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’d say so. The house has gotten pretty hard for my wife and me to live in. I’ve got materials and half-finished things taking over rooms we actually need. And… people I care about are staying away from me now after the arguments. So yeah, it’s gotten in the way.\n\nDoctor: At any time in your life, have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day, or lost interest or pleasure in almost everything?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had that. I’ve had rough days here and there, but I can’t remember two weeks straight where I was depressed most of the day or lost interest in almost everything.\n\nDoctor: Before this recent episode, have you ever had another time when you felt unusually high, energized, or irritable in a way similar to this?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Nothing comes to mind that was like this before. I may have had times I was more worked up or had more energy, but I can’t say it was the same as what’s been going on recently.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
